# Research Group subset-b-000606

Grouped research for ceph-client Linux Devicetree binding YAML schemas covering power-supply devices, PowerPC platform power management, PPS/PTP time sources, PWM controllers, and regulator devices. Each source file section is delimited for deterministic splitting into source-tree-aligned per-file research documents.

# sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/power/supply/lltc,ltc294x.yaml

## Purpose
`sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/power/supply/lltc,ltc294x.yaml` defines the Linux Devicetree YAML schema for `LTC2941, LTC2942, LTC2943 and LTC2944 battery fuel gauges`, a battery, fuel-gauge, or battery-monitor binding. The schema description says: All chips measure battery capacity. The LTC2942 is pin compatible with the LTC2941, it adds voltage and temperature monitoring, and is runtime detected. LTC2943 and LTC2944 are software compatible, uses a slightly different conversion formula for the charge counter and adds voltage, current and temperature monitoring. It is declarative ABI documentation and validation input, not executable driver code. Its job is to keep DTS/DTB nodes aligned with the resources, child nodes, and electrical or timing constraints expected by the matching kernel subsystem before a driver probes hardware.

## Important APIs, Types, and Functions
The API surface is the schema contract: `$id`, `$schema`, `title`, `maintainers`, `properties`, `patternProperties`, `required`, composition keys, examples, and referenced common schemas. `compatible` uses an `enum` with 4 tokens: `lltc,ltc2941`, `lltc,ltc2942`, `lltc,ltc2943`, `lltc,ltc2944`. Top-level properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `lltc,resistor-sense`, `lltc,prescaler-exponent`. Top-level required properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `lltc,resistor-sense`, `lltc,prescaler-exponent`; required properties collected from nested branches include `compatible`, `lltc,prescaler-exponent`, `lltc,resistor-sense`, `reg`. Patterned child schemas are none. Numeric and collection constraints include `maxItems=1`. Important resource roles: `compatible` is the stable hardware ABI and OF match key; `reg` gives MMIO, I2C, SPI, child address, or selector placement. The highest-risk ABI details are compatible identity, I2C/MMIO address, battery phandle, monitor resistor/calibration values, alert/IRQ wiring, charge/voltage/current limits, and vendor battery profile data.

## Control Flow
Control flow is dt-schema evaluation rather than imperative execution. `dt_binding_check` loads the core meta-schema, resolves `$ref` entries, applies `properties` and `patternProperties`, evaluates `allOf`, checks required fields and min/max constraints, validates examples, and enforces the closure policy. At runtime this YAML does not execute: the compiled DTB node is matched through a kernel OF table or subsystem-specific child-node parser, then helpers for power-supply, regulator, PWM, PTP, PPS, PowerPC platform, clocks, resets, interrupts, GPIOs, pinctrl, power domains, and MFD parents consume the validated resources.

## State and Persistence Behavior
The file stores no mutable runtime state and writes no persistent data. Its persistence is ABI stability: compatible strings, required resource names, phandle/cell counts, child-node names, electrical limit properties, fallback ordering, and example layouts become long-lived contracts in DTS and DTB artifacts. Runtime state belongs to drivers and hardware after probe, including power_supply registration, state-of-charge, voltage/current/temperature measurements, battery health, charge counters, alert thresholds, suspend/resume sampling, and cached gauge registers.

## Dependencies and Integration Points
Maintainers listed: Sebastian Reichel <sre@kernel.org>. Direct schema dependencies are `/schemas/types.yaml#/definitions/int32`, `/schemas/types.yaml#/definitions/uint32`, `power-supply.yaml#`. Power-supply integration is through `drivers/power/supply`, `power_supply` properties exported to userspace, charger-manager users, thermal hooks, and board DTS battery descriptions. Broader integration points include the Linux power_supply class, I2C/platform fuel-gauge drivers, battery-info phandles, thermistor/temperature handling, IRQs, regulators, and charger coordination. Driver integration is through OF `compatible` matching and subsystem helpers; board integration is through DTS users that instantiate the compatible strings, child rails/channels, and phandle topology. Compatible scan signals found driver-side files: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/power/supply/ltc2941-battery-gauge.c`. In-tree DTS scan signals found board users: no in-tree DTS users found by compatible scan. The file includes 1 embedded example for dt-schema validation.

## Risks
Primary risks are Devicetree ABI drift, incomplete compatible coverage, wrong fallback ordering, and resource-shape changes that still compile but break driver probe, power sequencing, clock setup, IRQ delivery, or consumer phandle resolution. Tightening required properties can reject existing board files; loosening them can hide missing supplies, clocks, resets, interrupts, current/voltage limits, PWM cell counts, PTP/PPS GPIOs, or regulator child constraints. This schema rejects unknown top-level properties with `additionalProperties: false`. It has 4 top-level properties and 4 distinct nested property names, so broad edits should be checked against all compatibles, examples, and in-tree DTS users rather than only the filename's nominal device.

## Test Signals
Run `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/power/supply/lltc,ltc294x.yaml` for targeted schema validation and `make dtbs_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/power/supply/lltc,ltc294x.yaml` against boards using these nodes. High-value negative checks remove required `compatible`, `reg`, interrupts, clocks, resets, supplies, GPIOs, child regulator/PWM/battery nodes, or phandles; use invalid cell counts or child `reg` values; misspell vendor properties; and exercise every compatible-specific branch. Runtime integration signals are successful driver probe, correct power_supply/regulator/PWM/PTP/PPS/platform registration, clean suspend/resume or runtime-PM behavior where applicable, valid interrupt/reset/power sequencing, and no dtbs_check regressions in embedded examples or in-tree DTS users.
<!-- END_FILE_RESEARCH: sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/power/supply/lltc,ltc294x.yaml -->

# sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/power/supply/maxim,ds2760.yaml

## Purpose
`sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/power/supply/maxim,ds2760.yaml` defines the Linux Devicetree YAML schema for `Maxim DS2760`, a power-supply common-schema or device binding. The schema description says: The ds2760 is a w1 slave device and must hence have its sub-node in DT under a w1 bus master node. It is declarative ABI documentation and validation input, not executable driver code. Its job is to keep DTS/DTB nodes aligned with the resources, child nodes, and electrical or timing constraints expected by the matching kernel subsystem before a driver probes hardware.

## Important APIs, Types, and Functions
The API surface is the schema contract: `$id`, `$schema`, `title`, `maintainers`, `properties`, `patternProperties`, `required`, composition keys, examples, and referenced common schemas. `compatible` uses a single `const` with 1 token: `maxim,ds2760`. Top-level properties are `compatible`, `maxim,pmod-enabled`, `maxim,cache-time-ms`, `rated-capacity-microamp-hours`. Top-level required properties are `compatible`; required properties collected from nested branches include `compatible`. Patterned child schemas are none. Numeric and collection constraints include no explicit numeric or item limits collected. Important resource roles: `compatible` is the stable hardware ABI and OF match key; `rated-capacity-microamp-hours` constrains electrical limits or measurement calibration. The highest-risk ABI details are common property naming, supply relationships, battery or charger child-node layout, measurement/calibration values, and cross-device phandle contracts.

## Control Flow
Control flow is dt-schema evaluation rather than imperative execution. `dt_binding_check` loads the core meta-schema, resolves `$ref` entries, applies `properties` and `patternProperties`, evaluates `allOf`, checks required fields and min/max constraints, validates examples, and enforces the closure policy. At runtime this YAML does not execute: the compiled DTB node is matched through a kernel OF table or subsystem-specific child-node parser, then helpers for power-supply, regulator, PWM, PTP, PPS, PowerPC platform, clocks, resets, interrupts, GPIOs, pinctrl, power domains, and MFD parents consume the validated resources.

## State and Persistence Behavior
The file stores no mutable runtime state and writes no persistent data. Its persistence is ABI stability: compatible strings, required resource names, phandle/cell counts, child-node names, electrical limit properties, fallback ordering, and example layouts become long-lived contracts in DTS and DTB artifacts. Runtime state belongs to drivers and hardware after probe, including reported online/status/health/capacity/voltage/current/temperature state and any driver-private cached measurement state.

## Dependencies and Integration Points
Maintainers listed: Sebastian Reichel <sre@kernel.org>. Direct schema dependencies are `power-supply.yaml#`. Integration is through common `power-supply.yaml`, vendor charger/fuel-gauge bindings, `drivers/power/supply`, userspace power-supply attributes, and board DTS power topology. Broader integration points include the Linux power_supply class, battery/charger/AC/USB supply providers, battery-info schemas, regulators, GPIOs, interrupts, thermal zones, and parent PMIC/MFD nodes. Driver integration is through OF `compatible` matching and subsystem helpers; board integration is through DTS users that instantiate the compatible strings, child rails/channels, and phandle topology. Compatible scan signals found driver-side files: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/power/supply/ds2760_battery.c`. In-tree DTS scan signals found board users: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/intel/pxa/pxa300-raumfeld-controller.dts`. The file includes 0 embedded examples for dt-schema validation.

## Risks
Primary risks are Devicetree ABI drift, incomplete compatible coverage, wrong fallback ordering, and resource-shape changes that still compile but break driver probe, power sequencing, clock setup, IRQ delivery, or consumer phandle resolution. Tightening required properties can reject existing board files; loosening them can hide missing supplies, clocks, resets, interrupts, current/voltage limits, PWM cell counts, PTP/PPS GPIOs, or regulator child constraints. This schema closes composed schemas with `unevaluatedProperties: false`. It has 4 top-level properties and 4 distinct nested property names, so broad edits should be checked against all compatibles, examples, and in-tree DTS users rather than only the filename's nominal device.

## Test Signals
Run `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/power/supply/maxim,ds2760.yaml` for targeted schema validation and `make dtbs_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/power/supply/maxim,ds2760.yaml` against boards using these nodes. High-value negative checks remove required `compatible`, `reg`, interrupts, clocks, resets, supplies, GPIOs, child regulator/PWM/battery nodes, or phandles; use invalid cell counts or child `reg` values; misspell vendor properties; and exercise every compatible-specific branch. Runtime integration signals are successful driver probe, correct power_supply/regulator/PWM/PTP/PPS/platform registration, clean suspend/resume or runtime-PM behavior where applicable, valid interrupt/reset/power sequencing, and no dtbs_check regressions in embedded examples or in-tree DTS users.

# sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/power/supply/maxim,max14656.yaml

## Purpose
`sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/power/supply/maxim,max14656.yaml` defines the Linux Devicetree YAML schema for `Maxim MAX14656`, a power-supply common-schema or device binding. It is declarative ABI documentation and validation input, not executable driver code. Its job is to keep DTS/DTB nodes aligned with the resources, child nodes, and electrical or timing constraints expected by the matching kernel subsystem before a driver probes hardware.

## Important APIs, Types, and Functions
The API surface is the schema contract: `$id`, `$schema`, `title`, `maintainers`, `properties`, `patternProperties`, `required`, composition keys, examples, and referenced common schemas. `compatible` uses a single `const` with 1 token: `maxim,max14656`. Top-level properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `interrupts`. Top-level required properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `interrupts`; required properties collected from nested branches include `compatible`, `interrupts`, `reg`. Patterned child schemas are none. Numeric and collection constraints include `maxItems=1`. Important resource roles: `compatible` is the stable hardware ABI and OF match key; `reg` gives MMIO, I2C, SPI, child address, or selector placement; `interrupts` routes alert, status, protection, capture, timer, or controller IRQs. The highest-risk ABI details are common property naming, supply relationships, battery or charger child-node layout, measurement/calibration values, and cross-device phandle contracts.

## Control Flow
Control flow is dt-schema evaluation rather than imperative execution. `dt_binding_check` loads the core meta-schema, resolves `$ref` entries, applies `properties` and `patternProperties`, evaluates `allOf`, checks required fields and min/max constraints, validates examples, and enforces the closure policy. At runtime this YAML does not execute: the compiled DTB node is matched through a kernel OF table or subsystem-specific child-node parser, then helpers for power-supply, regulator, PWM, PTP, PPS, PowerPC platform, clocks, resets, interrupts, GPIOs, pinctrl, power domains, and MFD parents consume the validated resources.

## State and Persistence Behavior
The file stores no mutable runtime state and writes no persistent data. Its persistence is ABI stability: compatible strings, required resource names, phandle/cell counts, child-node names, electrical limit properties, fallback ordering, and example layouts become long-lived contracts in DTS and DTB artifacts. Runtime state belongs to drivers and hardware after probe, including reported online/status/health/capacity/voltage/current/temperature state and any driver-private cached measurement state.

## Dependencies and Integration Points
Maintainers listed: Sebastian Reichel <sre@kernel.org>. Direct schema dependencies are `power-supply.yaml#`. Integration is through common `power-supply.yaml`, vendor charger/fuel-gauge bindings, `drivers/power/supply`, userspace power-supply attributes, and board DTS power topology. Broader integration points include the Linux power_supply class, battery/charger/AC/USB supply providers, battery-info schemas, regulators, GPIOs, interrupts, thermal zones, and parent PMIC/MFD nodes. Driver integration is through OF `compatible` matching and subsystem helpers; board integration is through DTS users that instantiate the compatible strings, child rails/channels, and phandle topology. Compatible scan signals found driver-side files: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/power/supply/max14656_charger_detector.c`. In-tree DTS scan signals found board users: no in-tree DTS users found by compatible scan. The file includes 1 embedded example for dt-schema validation.

## Risks
Primary risks are Devicetree ABI drift, incomplete compatible coverage, wrong fallback ordering, and resource-shape changes that still compile but break driver probe, power sequencing, clock setup, IRQ delivery, or consumer phandle resolution. Tightening required properties can reject existing board files; loosening them can hide missing supplies, clocks, resets, interrupts, current/voltage limits, PWM cell counts, PTP/PPS GPIOs, or regulator child constraints. This schema rejects unknown top-level properties with `additionalProperties: false`. It has 3 top-level properties and 3 distinct nested property names, so broad edits should be checked against all compatibles, examples, and in-tree DTS users rather than only the filename's nominal device.

## Test Signals
Run `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/power/supply/maxim,max14656.yaml` for targeted schema validation and `make dtbs_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/power/supply/maxim,max14656.yaml` against boards using these nodes. High-value negative checks remove required `compatible`, `reg`, interrupts, clocks, resets, supplies, GPIOs, child regulator/PWM/battery nodes, or phandles; use invalid cell counts or child `reg` values; misspell vendor properties; and exercise every compatible-specific branch. Runtime integration signals are successful driver probe, correct power_supply/regulator/PWM/PTP/PPS/platform registration, clean suspend/resume or runtime-PM behavior where applicable, valid interrupt/reset/power sequencing, and no dtbs_check regressions in embedded examples or in-tree DTS users.

## Dependencies and Integration Points
Maintainers listed: Krzysztof Kozlowski <krzk@kernel.org>. Direct schema dependencies are dt-schema core/meta schemas only. Integration is through `drivers/power/supply`, MFD/PMIC parent nodes, `power_supply` class devices, charger-manager policy, USB/extcon detection paths, and DTS board power trees. Broader integration points include the Linux power_supply class, charger drivers, USB/AC input detection, extcon/USB Type-C or BC1.2 integrations, regulators, IRQs, GPIO enables, and battery phandles. Driver integration is through OF `compatible` matching and subsystem helpers; board integration is through DTS users that instantiate the compatible strings, child rails/channels, and phandle topology. Compatible scan signals found driver-side files: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/mfd/max77693.c`. In-tree DTS scan signals found board users: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/samsung/exynos4212-tab3.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/samsung/exynos4412-midas.dtsi`. The file includes 0 embedded examples for dt-schema validation.

## Risks
Primary risks are Devicetree ABI drift, incomplete compatible coverage, wrong fallback ordering, and resource-shape changes that still compile but break driver probe, power sequencing, clock setup, IRQ delivery, or consumer phandle resolution. Tightening required properties can reject existing board files; loosening them can hide missing supplies, clocks, resets, interrupts, current/voltage limits, PWM cell counts, PTP/PPS GPIOs, or regulator child constraints. This schema rejects unknown top-level properties with `additionalProperties: false`. It has 6 top-level properties and 6 distinct nested property names, so broad edits should be checked against all compatibles, examples, and in-tree DTS users rather than only the filename's nominal device.

## Test Signals
Run `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/power/supply/maxim,max77693.yaml` for targeted schema validation and `make dtbs_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/power/supply/maxim,max77693.yaml` against boards using these nodes. High-value negative checks remove required `compatible`, `reg`, interrupts, clocks, resets, supplies, GPIOs, child regulator/PWM/battery nodes, or phandles; use invalid cell counts or child `reg` values; misspell vendor properties; and exercise every compatible-specific branch. Runtime integration signals are successful driver probe, correct power_supply/regulator/PWM/PTP/PPS/platform registration, clean suspend/resume or runtime-PM behavior where applicable, valid interrupt/reset/power sequencing, and no dtbs_check regressions in embedded examples or in-tree DTS users.

The API surface is the schema contract: `$id`, `$schema`, `title`, `maintainers`, `properties`, `patternProperties`, `required`, composition keys, examples, and referenced common schemas. `compatible` uses a single `const` with 1 token: `maxim,max77705-charger`. # sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/power/supply/microchip,ucs1002.yaml

## Purpose
`sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/power/supply/microchip,ucs1002.yaml` defines the Linux Devicetree YAML schema for `Microchip UCS1002 USB Port Power Controller`, a power-supply common-schema or device binding. It is declarative ABI documentation and validation input, not executable driver code. Its job is to keep DTS/DTB nodes aligned with the resources, child nodes, and electrical or timing constraints expected by the matching kernel subsystem before a driver probes hardware.

## Important APIs, Types, and Functions
The API surface is the schema contract: `$id`, `$schema`, `title`, `maintainers`, `properties`, `patternProperties`, `required`, composition keys, examples, and referenced common schemas. `compatible` uses a single `const` with 1 token: `microchip,ucs1002`. Top-level properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `interrupts`, `interrupt-names`. Top-level required properties are `compatible`, `reg`; required properties collected from nested branches include `compatible`, `reg`. Patterned child schemas are none. Numeric and collection constraints include `maxItems=1`, `maxItems=2`. Important resource roles: `compatible` is the stable hardware ABI and OF match key; `reg` gives MMIO, I2C, SPI, child address, or selector placement; `interrupts` routes alert, status, protection, capture, timer, or controller IRQs; `interrupt-names` routes alert, status, protection, capture, timer, or controller IRQs. The highest-risk ABI details are common property naming, supply relationships, battery or charger child-node layout, measurement/calibration values, and cross-device phandle contracts.

## Control Flow
Control flow is dt-schema evaluation rather than imperative execution. `dt_binding_check` loads the core meta-schema, resolves `$ref` entries, applies `properties` and `patternProperties`, evaluates no top-level conditionals, checks required fields and min/max constraints, validates examples, and enforces the closure policy. At runtime this YAML does not execute: the compiled DTB node is matched through a kernel OF table or subsystem-specific child-node parser, then helpers for power-supply, regulator, PWM, PTP, PPS, PowerPC platform, clocks, resets, interrupts, GPIOs, pinctrl, power domains, and MFD parents consume the validated resources.

## State and Persistence Behavior
The file stores no mutable runtime state and writes no persistent data. Its persistence is ABI stability: compatible strings, required resource names, phandle/cell counts, child-node names, electrical limit properties, fallback ordering, and example layouts become long-lived contracts in DTS and DTB artifacts. Runtime state belongs to drivers and hardware after probe, including reported online/status/health/capacity/voltage/current/temperature state and any driver-private cached measurement state.

## Dependencies and Integration Points
Maintainers listed: Sebastian Reichel <sre@kernel.org>. Direct schema dependencies are dt-schema core/meta schemas only. Integration is through common `power-supply.yaml`, vendor charger/fuel-gauge bindings, `drivers/power/supply`, userspace power-supply attributes, and board DTS power topology. Broader integration points include the Linux power_supply class, battery/charger/AC/USB supply providers, battery-info schemas, regulators, GPIOs, interrupts, thermal zones, and parent PMIC/MFD nodes. Driver integration is through OF `compatible` matching and subsystem helpers; board integration is through DTS users that instantiate the compatible strings, child rails/channels, and phandle topology. The file includes 1 embedded example for dt-schema validation.

## Risks
Primary risks are Devicetree ABI drift, incomplete compatible coverage, wrong fallback ordering, and resource-shape changes that still compile but break driver probe, power sequencing, clock setup, IRQ delivery, or consumer phandle resolution. Tightening required properties can reject existing board files; loosening them can hide missing supplies, clocks, resets, interrupts, current/voltage limits, PWM cell counts, PTP/PPS GPIOs, or regulator child constraints. This schema rejects unknown top-level properties with `additionalProperties: false`. It has 4 top-level properties and 4 distinct nested property names, so broad edits should be checked against all compatibles, examples, and in-tree DTS users rather than only the filename's nominal device.

# sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/power/supply/pegatron,chagall-ec.yaml

## Purpose
`sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/power/supply/pegatron,chagall-ec.yaml` defines the Linux Devicetree YAML schema for `Pegatron Chagall EC`, a power-supply common-schema or device binding. The schema description says: Pegatron Chagall EC is based on an 8-bit programmable microcontroller from Infineon/Cypress Semiconductor, it communicates over I2C and is used in the Pegatron Chagall tablet for fuel gauge and battery control functions. It is declarative ABI documentation and validation input, not executable driver code. Its job is to keep DTS/DTB nodes aligned with the resources, child nodes, and electrical or timing constraints expected by the matching kernel subsystem before a driver probes hardware.

## Important APIs, Types, and Functions
The API surface is the schema contract: `$id`, `$schema`, `title`, `maintainers`, `properties`, `patternProperties`, `required`, composition keys, examples, and referenced common schemas. `compatible` uses a single `const` with 1 token: `pegatron,chagall-ec`. Top-level properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `monitored-battery`, `power-supplies`. Top-level required properties are `compatible`, `reg`; required properties collected from nested branches include `compatible`, `reg`. Patterned child schemas are none. Numeric and collection constraints include `maxItems=1`. Important resource roles: `compatible` is the stable hardware ABI and OF match key; `reg` gives MMIO, I2C, SPI, child address, or selector placement; `monitored-battery` connects battery, charger, or supply provider relationships; `power-supplies` connects battery, charger, or supply provider relationships. The highest-risk ABI details are common property naming, supply relationships, battery or charger child-node layout, measurement/calibration values, and cross-device phandle contracts.

## Control Flow
Control flow is dt-schema evaluation rather than imperative execution. `dt_binding_check` loads the core meta-schema, resolves `$ref` entries, applies `properties` and `patternProperties`, evaluates no top-level conditionals, checks required fields and min/max constraints, validates examples, and enforces the closure policy. At runtime this YAML does not execute: the compiled DTB node is matched through a kernel OF table or subsystem-specific child-node parser, then helpers for power-supply, regulator, PWM, PTP, PPS, PowerPC platform, clocks, resets, interrupts, GPIOs, pinctrl, power domains, and MFD parents consume the validated resources.

## State and Persistence Behavior
The file stores no mutable runtime state and writes no persistent data. Its persistence is ABI stability: compatible strings, required resource names, phandle/cell counts, child-node names, electrical limit properties, fallback ordering, and example layouts become long-lived contracts in DTS and DTB artifacts. Runtime state belongs to drivers and hardware after probe, including reported online/status/health/capacity/voltage/current/temperature state and any driver-private cached measurement state.

## Dependencies and Integration Points
Maintainers listed: Svyatoslav Ryhel <clamor95@gmail.com>. Direct schema dependencies are `/schemas/power/supply/power-supply.yaml`. Integration is through common `power-supply.yaml`, vendor charger/fuel-gauge bindings, `drivers/power/supply`, userspace power-supply attributes, and board DTS power topology. Broader integration points include the Linux power_supply class, battery/charger/AC/USB supply providers, battery-info schemas, regulators, GPIOs, interrupts, thermal zones, and parent PMIC/MFD nodes. Driver integration is through OF `compatible` matching and subsystem helpers; board integration is through DTS users that instantiate the compatible strings, child rails/channels, and phandle topology. In-tree DTS scan signals found board users: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/nvidia/tegra30-pegatron-chagall.dts`. The file includes 1 embedded example for dt-schema validation.

## Risks
Primary risks are Devicetree ABI drift, incomplete compatible coverage, wrong fallback ordering, and resource-shape changes that still compile but break driver probe, power sequencing, clock setup, IRQ delivery, or consumer phandle resolution. Tightening required properties can reject existing board files; loosening them can hide missing supplies, clocks, resets, interrupts, current/voltage limits, PWM cell counts, PTP/PPS GPIOs, or regulator child constraints. This schema closes composed schemas with `unevaluatedProperties: false`. It has 4 top-level properties and 4 distinct nested property names, so broad edits should be checked against all compatibles, examples, and in-tree DTS users rather than only the filename's nominal device.

## Test Signals
Run `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/power/supply/pegatron,chagall-ec.yaml` for targeted schema validation and `make dtbs_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/power/supply/pegatron,chagall-ec.yaml` against boards using these nodes. High-value negative checks remove required `compatible`, `reg`, interrupts, clocks, resets, supplies, GPIOs, child regulator/PWM/battery nodes, or phandles; use invalid cell counts or child `reg` values; misspell vendor properties; and exercise every compatible-specific branch. The file includes 1 embedded example for dt-schema validation.

## Risks
Primary risks are Devicetree ABI drift, incomplete compatible coverage, wrong fallback ordering, and resource-shape changes that still compile but break driver probe, power sequencing, clock setup, IRQ delivery, or consumer phandle resolution. Tightening required properties can reject existing board files; loosening them can hide missing supplies, clocks, resets, interrupts, current/voltage limits, PWM cell counts, PTP/PPS GPIOs, or regulator child constraints. This schema rejects unknown top-level properties with `additionalProperties: false`. It has 17 top-level properties and 17 distinct nested property names, so broad edits should be checked against all compatibles, examples, and in-tree DTS users rather than only the filename's nominal device.

## Test Signals
Run `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/power/supply/qcom,pm8941-charger.yaml` for targeted schema validation and `make dtbs_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/power/supply/qcom,pm8941-charger.yaml` against boards using these nodes. High-value negative checks remove required `compatible`, `reg`, interrupts, clocks, resets, supplies, GPIOs, child regulator/PWM/battery nodes, or phandles; use invalid cell counts or child `reg` values; misspell vendor properties; and exercise every compatible-specific branch. Runtime integration signals are successful driver probe, correct power_supply/regulator/PWM/PTP/PPS/platform registration, clean suspend/resume or runtime-PM behavior where applicable, valid interrupt/reset/power sequencing, and no dtbs_check regressions in embedded examples or in-tree DTS users.

## Purpose
`sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/power/supply/qcom,pm8941-coincell.yaml` defines the Linux Devicetree YAML schema for `Qualcomm Coincell Charger`, a charger or external-power-supply binding. The schema description says: The hardware block controls charging for a coincell or capacitor that is used to provide power backup for certain features of the power management IC (PMIC) It is declarative ABI documentation and validation input, not executable driver code. Its job is to keep DTS/DTB nodes aligned with the resources, child nodes, and electrical or timing constraints expected by the matching kernel subsystem before a driver probes hardware.

## Important APIs, Types, and Functions
The API surface is the schema contract: `$id`, `$schema`, `title`, `maintainers`, `properties`, `patternProperties`, `required`, composition keys, examples, and referenced common schemas. `compatible` uses `oneOf` with 2 branchs with 2 tokens: `qcom,pm8998-coincell`, `qcom,pm8941-coincell`. Numeric and collection constraints include `maxItems=1`. Important resource roles: `compatible` is the stable hardware ABI and OF match key; `reg` gives MMIO, I2C, SPI, child address, or selector placement; `qcom,charger-disable` connects battery, charger, or supply provider relationships. The highest-risk ABI details are charger compatible identity, input/charge current limits, voltage limits, power-path wiring, IRQ and GPIO semantics, battery references, and PMIC child-node placement.

The file stores no mutable runtime state and writes no persistent data. At runtime this YAML does not execute: the compiled DTB node is matched through a kernel OF table or subsystem-specific child-node parser, then helpers for power-supply, regulator, PWM, PTP, PPS, PowerPC platform, clocks, resets, interrupts, GPIOs, pinctrl, power domains, and MFD parents consume the validated resources.

## State and Persistence Behavior
The file stores no mutable runtime state and writes no persistent data. Its persistence is ABI stability: compatible strings, required resource names, phandle/cell counts, child-node names, electrical limit properties, fallback ordering, and example layouts become long-lived contracts in DTS and DTB artifacts. Runtime state belongs to drivers and hardware after probe, including power_supply registration, state-of-charge, voltage/current/temperature measurements, battery health, charge counters, alert thresholds, suspend/resume sampling, and cached gauge registers.

## Dependencies and Integration Points
Maintainers listed: Sebastian Reichel <sre@kernel.org>. Direct schema dependencies are `/schemas/types.yaml#/definitions/uint32`, `power-supply.yaml#`. ## Purpose
`sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/power/supply/richtek,rt9467.yaml` defines the Linux Devicetree YAML schema for `Richtek RT9467 Switching Battery Charger with Power Path Management`, a battery, fuel-gauge, or battery-monitor binding. The schema description says: RT9467 is a switch-mode single cell Li-Ion/Li-Polymer battery charger for portable applications. It integrates a synchronous PWM controller, power MOSFETs, input current sensing and regulation, high-accuracy voltage regulation, and charge termination. The charge current is regulated through integrated sensing resistors. The RT9467 also features USB On-The-Go (OTG) support. It also integrates D+/D- pin for USB host/charging port detection. Datasheet is available at https://www.richtek.com/assets/product_file/RT9467/DS9467-01.pdf It is declarative ABI documentation and validation input, not executable driver code. Patterned child schemas are none. Numeric and collection constraints include `maxItems=1`. # sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/power/supply/richtek,rt9756.yaml

## Purpose
`sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/power/supply/richtek,rt9756.yaml` defines the Linux Devicetree YAML schema for `Richtek RT9756 Smart Cap Divider Charger`, a charger or external-power-supply binding. The schema description says: The RT9756/RT9757 is a high efficiency and high charge current charger. The efficiency is up to 98.2% when VBAT = 4V, IBAT = 2A in DIV2 mode and 99.1% when VBAT=4V, IBAT=1A in bypass mode. The maximum charger current is up to 8A in DIV2 mode and 5A in bypass mode. The device integrates smart cap divider topology, direct charging mode, external over-voltage protection control, an input reverse blocking NFET and 2-way regulation, a dual phase charge pump core, 8-Channel high speed ADCs and USB BC 1.2 detection. RT9770 is almost the same with RT9756/57, only BC 1.2 detection function is removed to shrink the die... It is declarative ABI documentation and validation input, not executable driver code. Its job is to keep DTS/DTB nodes aligned with the resources, child nodes, and electrical or timing constraints expected by the matching kernel subsystem before a driver probes hardware.

## Important APIs, Types, and Functions
The API surface is the schema contract: `$id`, `$schema`, `title`, `maintainers`, `properties`, `patternProperties`, `required`, composition keys, examples, and referenced common schemas. `compatible` uses `oneOf` with 2 branchs with 3 tokens: `richtek,rt9756`, `richtek,rt9770`, `richtek,rt9757`. Top-level properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `wakeup-source`, `interrupts`, `shunt-resistor-micro-ohms`. Top-level required properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `interrupts`; required properties collected from nested branches include `compatible`, `interrupts`, `reg`. Patterned child schemas are none. Numeric and collection constraints include `maxItems=1`. Important resource roles: `compatible` is the stable hardware ABI and OF match key; `reg` gives MMIO, I2C, SPI, child address, or selector placement; `interrupts` routes alert, status, protection, capture, timer, or controller IRQs. The highest-risk ABI details are charger compatible identity, input/charge current limits, voltage limits, power-path wiring, IRQ and GPIO semantics, battery references, and PMIC child-node placement.

## Control Flow
Control flow is dt-schema evaluation rather than imperative execution. `dt_binding_check` loads the core meta-schema, resolves `$ref` entries, applies `properties` and `patternProperties`, evaluates `allOf`, checks required fields and min/max constraints, validates examples, and enforces the closure policy. At runtime this YAML does not execute: the compiled DTB node is matched through a kernel OF table or subsystem-specific child-node parser, then helpers for power-supply, regulator, PWM, PTP, PPS, PowerPC platform, clocks, resets, interrupts, GPIOs, pinctrl, power domains, and MFD parents consume the validated resources.

## State and Persistence Behavior
The file stores no mutable runtime state and writes no persistent data. Its persistence is ABI stability: compatible strings, required resource names, phandle/cell counts, child-node names, electrical limit properties, fallback ordering, and example layouts become long-lived contracts in DTS and DTB artifacts. Runtime state belongs to drivers and hardware after probe, including charger online/health/status state, input-current limiting, charge termination, watchdogs, USB/AC detection state, IRQ status, regulator enable state, and battery charging policy.

## Dependencies and Integration Points
Maintainers listed: ChiYuan Huang <cy_huang@richtek.com>. Direct schema dependencies are `power-supply.yaml#`. Integration is through `drivers/power/supply`, MFD/PMIC parent nodes, `power_supply` class devices, charger-manager policy, USB/extcon detection paths, and DTS board power trees. Broader integration points include the Linux power_supply class, charger drivers, USB/AC input detection, extcon/USB Type-C or BC1.2 integrations, regulators, IRQs, GPIO enables, and battery phandles. Driver integration is through OF `compatible` matching and subsystem helpers; board integration is through DTS users that instantiate the compatible strings, child rails/channels, and phandle topology. In-tree DTS scan signals found board users: no in-tree DTS users found by compatible scan. The file includes 1 embedded example for dt-schema validation.

## Risks
Primary risks are Devicetree ABI drift, incomplete compatible coverage, wrong fallback ordering, and resource-shape changes that still compile but break driver probe, power sequencing, clock setup, IRQ delivery, or consumer phandle resolution. Tightening required properties can reject existing board files; loosening them can hide missing supplies, clocks, resets, interrupts, current/voltage limits, PWM cell counts, PTP/PPS GPIOs, or regulator child constraints. This schema closes composed schemas with `unevaluatedProperties: false`. It has 5 top-level properties and 5 distinct nested property names, so broad edits should be checked against all compatibles, examples, and in-tree DTS users rather than only the filename's nominal device.

# sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/power/supply/samsung,battery.yaml

## Purpose
`sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/power/supply/samsung,battery.yaml` defines the Linux Devicetree YAML schema for `Samsung SDI Batteries`, a battery, fuel-gauge, or battery-monitor binding. The schema description says: Samsung SDI (Samsung Digital Interface) batteries are all different versions of lithium ion chemistry devices used for mobile phones, laptops and other portable electronics. The batteries are adapted to a specific product and the physical restrictions make it impossible to use another battery with the product, so product device trees can specify these batteries. Operating systems should determine hardware characteristics of the batteries from the compatible string. It is declarative ABI documentation and validation input, not executable driver code. Its job is to keep DTS/DTB nodes aligned with the resources, child nodes, and electrical or timing constraints expected by the matching kernel subsystem before a driver probes hardware.

## Important APIs, Types, and Functions

# sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/power/supply/sc2731-charger.yaml

## Purpose
`sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/power/supply/sc2731-charger.yaml` defines the Linux Devicetree YAML schema for `Spreadtrum SC2731 PMICs battery charger`, a battery, fuel-gauge, or battery-monitor binding. It is declarative ABI documentation and validation input, not executable driver code. Its job is to keep DTS/DTB nodes aligned with the resources, child nodes, and electrical or timing constraints expected by the matching kernel subsystem before a driver probes hardware.

## Important APIs, Types, and Functions
The API surface is the schema contract: `$id`, `$schema`, `title`, `maintainers`, `properties`, `patternProperties`, `required`, composition keys, examples, and referenced common schemas. `compatible` uses a single `const` with 1 token: `sprd,sc2731-charger`. Top-level properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `phys`, `monitored-battery`. Top-level required properties are none declared at the top level; required properties collected from nested branches include none found. Patterned child schemas are none. Numeric and collection constraints include `maxItems=1`. Important resource roles: `compatible` is the stable hardware ABI and OF match key; `reg` gives MMIO, I2C, SPI, child address, or selector placement; `monitored-battery` connects battery, charger, or supply provider relationships. The highest-risk ABI details are compatible identity, I2C/MMIO address, battery phandle, monitor resistor/calibration values, alert/IRQ wiring, charge/voltage/current limits, and vendor battery profile data.

## Control Flow
Control flow is dt-schema evaluation rather than imperative execution. `dt_binding_check` loads the core meta-schema, resolves `$ref` entries, applies `properties` and `patternProperties`, evaluates `allOf`, checks required fields and min/max constraints, validates examples, and enforces the closure policy. At runtime this YAML does not execute: the compiled DTB node is matched through a kernel OF table or subsystem-specific child-node parser, then helpers for power-supply, regulator, PWM, PTP, PPS, PowerPC platform, clocks, resets, interrupts, GPIOs, pinctrl, power domains, and MFD parents consume the validated resources.

## State and Persistence Behavior
The file stores no mutable runtime state and writes no persistent data. Its persistence is ABI stability: compatible strings, required resource names, phandle/cell counts, child-node names, electrical limit properties, fallback ordering, and example layouts become long-lived contracts in DTS and DTB artifacts. Runtime state belongs to drivers and hardware after probe, including power_supply registration, state-of-charge, voltage/current/temperature measurements, battery health, charge counters, alert thresholds, suspend/resume sampling, and cached gauge registers.

## Dependencies and Integration Points
Maintainers listed: Sebastian Reichel <sre@kernel.org>. Direct schema dependencies are `power-supply.yaml#`. Power-supply integration is through `drivers/power/supply`, `power_supply` properties exported to userspace, charger-manager users, thermal hooks, and board DTS battery descriptions. Broader integration points include the Linux power_supply class, I2C/platform fuel-gauge drivers, battery-info phandles, thermistor/temperature handling, IRQs, regulators, and charger coordination. Driver integration is through OF `compatible` matching and subsystem helpers; board integration is through DTS users that instantiate the compatible strings, child rails/channels, and phandle topology. Compatible scan signals found driver-side files: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/power/supply/sc2731_charger.c`. In-tree DTS scan signals found board users: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm64/boot/dts/sprd/sc2731.dtsi`. The file includes 0 embedded examples for dt-schema validation.

## Risks
Primary risks are Devicetree ABI drift, incomplete compatible coverage, wrong fallback ordering, and resource-shape changes that still compile but break driver probe, power sequencing, clock setup, IRQ delivery, or consumer phandle resolution. Tightening required properties can reject existing board files; loosening them can hide missing supplies, clocks, resets, interrupts, current/voltage limits, PWM cell counts, PTP/PPS GPIOs, or regulator child constraints. This schema rejects unknown top-level properties with `additionalProperties: false`. It has 4 top-level properties and 4 distinct nested property names, so broad edits should be checked against all compatibles, examples, and in-tree DTS users rather than only the filename's nominal device.

## Test Signals
Run `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/power/supply/sc2731-charger.yaml` for targeted schema validation and `make dtbs_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/power/supply/sc2731-charger.yaml` against boards using these nodes. High-value negative checks remove required `compatible`, `reg`, interrupts, clocks, resets, supplies, GPIOs, child regulator/PWM/battery nodes, or phandles; use invalid cell counts or child `reg` values; misspell vendor properties; and exercise every compatible-specific branch. Runtime integration signals are successful driver probe, correct power_supply/regulator/PWM/PTP/PPS/platform registration, clean suspend/resume or runtime-PM behavior where applicable, valid interrupt/reset/power sequencing, and no dtbs_check regressions in embedded examples or in-tree DTS users.
<!-- END_FILE_RESEARCH: sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/power/supply/sc2731-charger.yaml -->

# sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/power/supply/st,stc3117.yaml

## Purpose
`sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/power/supply/st,stc3117.yaml` defines the Linux Devicetree YAML schema for `STMicroelectronics STC3117 Fuel Gauge Unit Power Supply`, a battery, fuel-gauge, or battery-monitor binding. The schema description says: The STC3117 includes the STMicroelectronics OptimGauge algorithm. It provides accurate battery state-of-charge (SOC) monitoring, tracks battery parameter changes with operation conditions, temperature, and aging, and allows the application to get a battery state-of-health (SOH) indication. An alarm output signals low SOC or low voltage conditions and also indicates fault conditions like a missing or swapped battery. Datasheet is available at https://www.st.com/resource/en/datasheet/stc3117.pdf It is declarative ABI documentation and validation input, not executable driver code. Its job is to keep DTS/DTB nodes aligned with the resources, child nodes, and electrical or timing constraints expected by the matching kernel subsystem before a driver probes hardware.

Patterned child schemas are none. Numeric and collection constraints include `maxItems=1`. Important resource roles: `compatible` is the stable hardware ABI and OF match key; `reg` gives MMIO, I2C, SPI, child address, or selector placement; `monitored-battery` connects battery, charger, or supply provider relationships; `interrupts` routes alert, status, protection, capture, timer, or controller IRQs. The highest-risk ABI details are compatible identity, I2C/MMIO address, battery phandle, monitor resistor/calibration values, alert/IRQ wiring, charge/voltage/current limits, and vendor battery profile data.

Control flow is dt-schema evaluation rather than imperative execution. `dt_binding_check` loads the core meta-schema, resolves `$ref` entries, applies `properties` and `patternProperties`, evaluates `allOf`, checks required fields and min/max constraints, validates examples, and enforces the closure policy. Its persistence is ABI stability: compatible strings, required resource names, phandle/cell counts, child-node names, electrical limit properties, fallback ordering, and example layouts become long-lived contracts in DTS and DTB artifacts. # sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/power/supply/stericsson,ab8500-charger.yaml

## Purpose
`sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/power/supply/stericsson,ab8500-charger.yaml` defines the Linux Devicetree YAML schema for `AB8500 Charger`, a charger or external-power-supply binding. It is declarative ABI documentation and validation input, not executable driver code. Its job is to keep DTS/DTB nodes aligned with the resources, child nodes, and electrical or timing constraints expected by the matching kernel subsystem before a driver probes hardware.

## Important APIs, Types, and Functions
The API surface is the schema contract: `$id`, `$schema`, `title`, `maintainers`, `properties`, `patternProperties`, `required`, composition keys, examples, and referenced common schemas. `compatible` uses a single `const` with 1 token: `stericsson,ab8500-charger`. Top-level properties are `compatible`, `monitored-battery`, `battery`, `vddadc-supply`, `autopower_cfg`, `interrupts`, `interrupt-names`, `io-channels`, `io-channel-names`. Top-level required properties are `compatible`, `monitored-battery`, `vddadc-supply`, `interrupts`, `interrupt-names`, `io-channels`, `io-channel-names`; required properties collected from nested branches include `compatible`, `interrupt-names`, `interrupts`, `io-channel-names`, `io-channels`, `monitored-battery`, `vddadc-supply`. Patterned child schemas are none. Numeric and collection constraints include `maxItems=14`, `minItems=2`, `maxItems=4`. Important resource roles: `compatible` is the stable hardware ABI and OF match key; `monitored-battery` connects battery, charger, or supply provider relationships; `battery` connects battery, charger, or supply provider relationships; `vddadc-supply` links board power rails through the regulator framework; `interrupts` routes alert, status, protection, capture, timer, or controller IRQs; `interrupt-names` routes alert, status, protection, capture, timer, or controller IRQs. The highest-risk ABI details are charger compatible identity, input/charge current limits, voltage limits, power-path wiring, IRQ and GPIO semantics, battery references, and PMIC child-node placement.

## Control Flow
Control flow is dt-schema evaluation rather than imperative execution. `dt_binding_check` loads the core meta-schema, resolves `$ref` entries, applies `properties` and `patternProperties`, evaluates `allOf`, checks required fields and min/max constraints, validates examples, and enforces the closure policy. At runtime this YAML does not execute: the compiled DTB node is matched through a kernel OF table or subsystem-specific child-node parser, then helpers for power-supply, regulator, PWM, PTP, PPS, PowerPC platform, clocks, resets, interrupts, GPIOs, pinctrl, power domains, and MFD parents consume the validated resources.

## State and Persistence Behavior
The file stores no mutable runtime state and writes no persistent data. Its persistence is ABI stability: compatible strings, required resource names, phandle/cell counts, child-node names, electrical limit properties, fallback ordering, and example layouts become long-lived contracts in DTS and DTB artifacts. Runtime state belongs to drivers and hardware after probe, including charger online/health/status state, input-current limiting, charge termination, watchdogs, USB/AC detection state, IRQ status, regulator enable state, and battery charging policy.

## Dependencies and Integration Points
Maintainers listed: Sebastian Reichel <sre@kernel.org>. Direct schema dependencies are `/schemas/types.yaml#/definitions/phandle`, `power-supply.yaml#`. Integration is through `drivers/power/supply`, MFD/PMIC parent nodes, `power_supply` class devices, charger-manager policy, USB/extcon detection paths, and DTS board power trees. Broader integration points include the Linux power_supply class, charger drivers, USB/AC input detection, extcon/USB Type-C or BC1.2 integrations, regulators, IRQs, GPIO enables, and battery phandles. Driver integration is through OF `compatible` matching and subsystem helpers; board integration is through DTS users that instantiate the compatible strings, child rails/channels, and phandle topology. Compatible scan signals found driver-side files: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/mfd/ab8500-core.c`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/power/supply/ab8500_charger.c`. In-tree DTS scan signals found board users: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/st/ste-ab8500.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/st/ste-ab8505.dtsi`. The file includes 1 embedded example for dt-schema validation.

## Risks
Primary risks are Devicetree ABI drift, incomplete compatible coverage, wrong fallback ordering, and resource-shape changes that still compile but break driver probe, power sequencing, clock setup, IRQ delivery, or consumer phandle resolution. Tightening required properties can reject existing board files; loosening them can hide missing supplies, clocks, resets, interrupts, current/voltage limits, PWM cell counts, PTP/PPS GPIOs, or regulator child constraints. This schema rejects unknown top-level properties with `additionalProperties: false`. It has 9 top-level properties and 9 distinct nested property names, so broad edits should be checked against all compatibles, examples, and in-tree DTS users rather than only the filename's nominal device.

## Test Signals
Run `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/power/supply/stericsson,ab8500-charger.yaml` for targeted schema validation and `make dtbs_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/power/supply/stericsson,ab8500-charger.yaml` against boards using these nodes. High-value negative checks remove required `compatible`, `reg`, interrupts, clocks, resets, supplies, GPIOs, child regulator/PWM/battery nodes, or phandles; use invalid cell counts or child `reg` values; misspell vendor properties; and exercise every compatible-specific branch. Runtime integration signals are successful driver probe, correct power_supply/regulator/PWM/PTP/PPS/platform registration, clean suspend/resume or runtime-PM behavior where applicable, valid interrupt/reset/power sequencing, and no dtbs_check regressions in embedded examples or in-tree DTS users.
<!-- END_FILE_RESEARCH: sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/power/supply/stericsson,ab8500-charger.yaml -->

# sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/power/supply/twl4030-charger.yaml

## Purpose
`sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/power/supply/twl4030-charger.yaml` defines the Linux Devicetree YAML schema for `TWL4030 BCI (Battery Charger Interface)`, a battery, fuel-gauge, or battery-monitor binding. The schema description says: The battery charger needs to interact with the USB phy in order to know when charging is permissible, and when there is a connection or disconnection. The choice of phy cannot be configured at a hardware level, so there is no value in explicit configuration in device-tree. Rather if there is a sibling of the BCI node which is compatible with "ti,twl4030-usb", then that is used to determine when and how use USB power for charging. It is declarative ABI documentation and validation input, not executable driver code. # sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/powerpc/fsl/pmc.yaml

## Purpose
`sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/powerpc/fsl/pmc.yaml` defines the Linux Devicetree YAML schema for `Power Management Controller`, a PowerPC platform or power-management binding. The schema description says: The Power Management Controller in several MPC8xxx SoCs helps save power by controlling chip-wide low-power states as well as peripheral clock gating. Sleep of peripheral devices is configured by the `sleep` property, for example `sleep = <&pmc 0x00000030>`. Any cells after the &pmc phandle are called a sleep specifier. For "fsl,mpc8349-pmc", sleep specifiers consist of one cell. For each bit that is set in the cell, the corresponding bit in SCCR will be saved and cleared on suspend, and restored on resume. This sleep controller supports disabling and resuming devices at any time. For "fsl,mpc8536-pmc", sleep... It is declarative ABI documentation and validation input, not executable driver code. # sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/powerpc/sleep.yaml

## Purpose
`sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/powerpc/sleep.yaml` defines the Linux Devicetree YAML schema for `PowerPC sleep property`, a PowerPC platform or power-management binding. The schema description says: Devices on SOCs often have mechanisms for placing devices into low-power states that are decoupled from the devices' own register blocks. Sometimes, this information is more complicated than a cell-index property can reasonably describe. Thus, each device controlled in such a manner may contain a "sleep" property which describes these connections. The sleep property consists of one or more sleep resources, each of which consists of a phandle to a sleep controller, followed by a controller-specific sleep specifier of zero or more cells. At runtime this YAML does not execute: the compiled DTB node is matched through a kernel OF table or subsystem-specific child-node parser, then helpers for power-supply, regulator, PWM, PTP, PPS, PowerPC platform, clocks, resets, interrupts, GPIOs, pinctrl, power domains, and MFD parents consume the validated resources.

## State and Persistence Behavior
The file stores no mutable runtime state and writes no persistent data. Its persistence is ABI stability: compatible strings, required resource names, phandle/cell counts, child-node names, electrical limit properties, fallback ordering, and example layouts become long-lived contracts in DTS and DTB artifacts. Runtime state belongs to drivers and hardware after probe, including PHC registration, timecounter/cyclecounter state, external timestamp channels, periodic outputs, pin configuration, adjfine/adjtime state, and IRQ status.

## Dependencies and Integration Points
Maintainers listed: Min Li <min.li.xe@renesas.com>. Direct schema dependencies are dt-schema core/meta schemas only. Integration is through `drivers/ptp`, netdev timestamping paths, PTP class devices, clock providers, interrupt handling, and board DTS timing topology. Driver integration is through OF `compatible` matching and subsystem helpers; board integration is through DTS users that instantiate the compatible strings, child rails/channels, and phandle topology. Compatible scan signals found driver-side files: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/mfd/rsmu_i2c.c`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/mfd/rsmu_spi.c`. In-tree DTS scan signals found board users: no in-tree DTS users found by compatible scan. The file includes 1 embedded example for dt-schema validation.

## Risks
Primary risks are Devicetree ABI drift, incomplete compatible coverage, wrong fallback ordering, and resource-shape changes that still compile but break driver probe, power sequencing, clock setup, IRQ delivery, or consumer phandle resolution. Tightening required properties can reject existing board files; loosening them can hide missing supplies, clocks, resets, interrupts, current/voltage limits, PWM cell counts, PTP/PPS GPIOs, or regulator child constraints. This schema rejects unknown top-level properties with `additionalProperties: false`. It has 2 top-level properties and 2 distinct nested property names, so broad edits should be checked against all compatibles, examples, and in-tree DTS users rather than only the filename's nominal device.

## Test Signals
Run `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/ptp/ptp-idt82p33.yaml` for targeted schema validation and `make dtbs_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/ptp/ptp-idt82p33.yaml` against boards using these nodes. High-value negative checks remove required `compatible`, `reg`, interrupts, clocks, resets, supplies, GPIOs, child regulator/PWM/battery nodes, or phandles; use invalid cell counts or child `reg` values; misspell vendor properties; and exercise every compatible-specific branch. Runtime integration signals are successful driver probe, correct power_supply/regulator/PWM/PTP/PPS/platform registration, clean suspend/resume or runtime-PM behavior where applicable, valid interrupt/reset/power sequencing, and no dtbs_check regressions in embedded examples or in-tree DTS users.

Top-level required properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `clocks`; required properties collected from nested branches include `clocks`, `compatible`, `reg`. Patterned child schemas are none. Numeric and collection constraints include `maxItems=1`. Important resource roles: `compatible` is the stable hardware ABI and OF match key; `reg` gives MMIO, I2C, SPI, child address, or selector placement; `clocks` binds functional, bus, reference, timer, or output clocks; `power-domains` integrates the device with SoC PM, DMA, or IOMMU topology; `#pwm-cells` fixes child-node or phandle specifier shape. The highest-risk ABI details are compatible fallback ordering, register and clock layout, `#pwm-cells`, channel counts, period counter ranges, reset/interrupt resources, and SoC-specific polarity or prescaler rules.

## Control Flow
Control flow is dt-schema evaluation rather than imperative execution. `dt_binding_check` loads the core meta-schema, resolves `$ref` entries, applies `properties` and `patternProperties`, evaluates no top-level conditionals, checks required fields and min/max constraints, validates examples, and enforces the closure policy. At runtime this YAML does not execute: the compiled DTB node is matched through a kernel OF table or subsystem-specific child-node parser, then helpers for power-supply, regulator, PWM, PTP, PPS, PowerPC platform, clocks, resets, interrupts, GPIOs, pinctrl, power domains, and MFD parents consume the validated resources.

## State and Persistence Behavior
The file stores no mutable runtime state and writes no persistent data. Its persistence is ABI stability: compatible strings, required resource names, phandle/cell counts, child-node names, electrical limit properties, fallback ordering, and example layouts become long-lived contracts in DTS and DTB artifacts. Runtime state belongs to drivers and hardware after probe, including PWM chip registration, channel allocation, configured period/duty/polarity, enabled outputs, clock/runtime-PM state, and hardware counter register state.

Maintainers listed: asahi@lists.linux.dev, Sasha Finkelstein <k@chaosmail.tech>. Direct schema dependencies are dt-schema core/meta schemas only. ## Risks
Primary risks are Devicetree ABI drift, incomplete compatible coverage, wrong fallback ordering, and resource-shape changes that still compile but break driver probe, power sequencing, clock setup, IRQ delivery, or consumer phandle resolution. # sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/pwm/atmel,hlcdc-pwm.yaml

## Purpose
`sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/pwm/atmel,hlcdc-pwm.yaml` defines the Linux Devicetree YAML schema for `Atmel's HLCDC's PWM controller`, a PWM controller binding. The schema description says: The LCDC integrates a Pulse Width Modulation (PWM) Controller. This block generates the LCD contrast control signal (LCD_PWM) that controls the display's contrast by software. LCDC_PWM is an 8-bit PWM signal that can be converted to an analog voltage with a simple passive filter. LCD display panels have different backlight specifications in terms of minimum/maximum values for PWM frequency. If the LCDC PWM frequency range does not match the LCD display panel, it is possible to use the standalone PWM Controller to drive the backlight. It is declarative ABI documentation and validation input, not executable driver code. ## Control Flow

## State and Persistence Behavior
The file stores no mutable runtime state and writes no persistent data. Its persistence is ABI stability: compatible strings, required resource names, phandle/cell counts, child-node names, electrical limit properties, fallback ordering, and example layouts become long-lived contracts in DTS and DTB artifacts. Runtime state belongs to drivers and hardware after probe, including PWM chip registration, channel allocation, configured period/duty/polarity, enabled outputs, clock/runtime-PM state, and hardware counter register state.

This schema rejects unknown top-level properties with `additionalProperties: false`. It has 4 top-level properties and 4 distinct nested property names, so broad edits should be checked against all compatibles, examples, and in-tree DTS users rather than only the filename's nominal device.

## Test Signals
Run `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/pwm/brcm,bcm7038-pwm.yaml` for targeted schema validation and `make dtbs_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/pwm/brcm,bcm7038-pwm.yaml` against boards using these nodes. High-value negative checks remove required `compatible`, `reg`, interrupts, clocks, resets, supplies, GPIOs, child regulator/PWM/battery nodes, or phandles; use invalid cell counts or child `reg` values; misspell vendor properties; and exercise every compatible-specific branch. Runtime integration signals are successful driver probe, correct power_supply/regulator/PWM/PTP/PPS/platform registration, clean suspend/resume or runtime-PM behavior where applicable, valid interrupt/reset/power sequencing, and no dtbs_check regressions in embedded examples or in-tree DTS users.

Maintainers listed: Frank Li <Frank.Li@nxp.com>. Direct schema dependencies are `/schemas/types.yaml#/definitions/flag`, `pwm.yaml#`. # sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/pwm/google,cros-ec-pwm.yaml

## Purpose
`sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/pwm/google,cros-ec-pwm.yaml` defines the Linux Devicetree YAML schema for `PWM controlled by ChromeOS EC`, a PWM controller binding. The schema description says: Google's ChromeOS EC PWM is a simple PWM attached to the Embedded Controller (EC) and controlled via a host-command interface. An EC PWM node should be only found as a sub-node of the EC node (see Documentation/devicetree/bindings/embedded-controller/google,cros-ec.yaml). It is declarative ABI documentation and validation input, not executable driver code. Its job is to keep DTS/DTB nodes aligned with the resources, child nodes, and electrical or timing constraints expected by the matching kernel subsystem before a driver probes hardware.

## Important APIs, Types, and Functions
The API surface is the schema contract: `$id`, `$schema`, `title`, `maintainers`, `properties`, `patternProperties`, `required`, composition keys, examples, and referenced common schemas. `compatible` uses `oneOf` with 2 branchs with 2 tokens: `google,cros-ec-pwm`, `google,cros-ec-pwm-type`. Top-level properties are `compatible`, `#pwm-cells`. Top-level required properties are `compatible`; required properties collected from nested branches include `compatible`. Patterned child schemas are none. Numeric and collection constraints include no explicit numeric or item limits collected. Important resource roles: `compatible` is the stable hardware ABI and OF match key; `#pwm-cells` fixes child-node or phandle specifier shape. The highest-risk ABI details are compatible fallback ordering, register and clock layout, `#pwm-cells`, channel counts, period counter ranges, reset/interrupt resources, and SoC-specific polarity or prescaler rules.

## Control Flow
Control flow is dt-schema evaluation rather than imperative execution. `dt_binding_check` loads the core meta-schema, resolves `$ref` entries, applies `properties` and `patternProperties`, evaluates `allOf`, checks required fields and min/max constraints, validates examples, and enforces the closure policy. At runtime this YAML does not execute: the compiled DTB node is matched through a kernel OF table or subsystem-specific child-node parser, then helpers for power-supply, regulator, PWM, PTP, PPS, PowerPC platform, clocks, resets, interrupts, GPIOs, pinctrl, power domains, and MFD parents consume the validated resources.

## State and Persistence Behavior
The file stores no mutable runtime state and writes no persistent data. Its persistence is ABI stability: compatible strings, required resource names, phandle/cell counts, child-node names, electrical limit properties, fallback ordering, and example layouts become long-lived contracts in DTS and DTB artifacts. Runtime state belongs to drivers and hardware after probe, including PWM chip registration, channel allocation, configured period/duty/polarity, enabled outputs, clock/runtime-PM state, and hardware counter register state.

## Dependencies and Integration Points
Maintainers listed: Thierry Reding <thierry.reding@gmail.com>, "Uwe Kleine-König" <u.kleine-koenig@pengutronix.de>. Direct schema dependencies are `pwm.yaml#`. # sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/pwm/intel,lgm-pwm.yaml

## Purpose
`sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/pwm/intel,lgm-pwm.yaml` defines the Linux Devicetree YAML schema for `LGM SoC PWM fan controller`, a PWM-driven fan or controller binding. It is declarative ABI documentation and validation input, not executable driver code. Its job is to keep DTS/DTB nodes aligned with the resources, child nodes, and electrical or timing constraints expected by the matching kernel subsystem before a driver probes hardware.

## Important APIs, Types, and Functions
The API surface is the schema contract: `$id`, `$schema`, `title`, `maintainers`, `properties`, `patternProperties`, `required`, composition keys, examples, and referenced common schemas. `compatible` uses a single `const` with 1 token: `intel,lgm-pwm`. Top-level properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `#pwm-cells`, `clocks`, `resets`. Top-level required properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `clocks`, `resets`; required properties collected from nested branches include `clocks`, `compatible`, `reg`, `resets`. Patterned child schemas are none. Numeric and collection constraints include `maxItems=1`. Important resource roles: `compatible` is the stable hardware ABI and OF match key; `reg` gives MMIO, I2C, SPI, child address, or selector placement; `#pwm-cells` fixes child-node or phandle specifier shape; `clocks` binds functional, bus, reference, timer, or output clocks; `resets` defines reset-controller or GPIO reset sequencing. The highest-risk ABI details are PWM provider details, fan GPIOs, cooling-level tables, tachometer or enable resources, and consumer phandle shape.

## Control Flow
Control flow is dt-schema evaluation rather than imperative execution. `dt_binding_check` loads the core meta-schema, resolves `$ref` entries, applies `properties` and `patternProperties`, evaluates `allOf`, checks required fields and min/max constraints, validates examples, and enforces the closure policy. At runtime this YAML does not execute: the compiled DTB node is matched through a kernel OF table or subsystem-specific child-node parser, then helpers for power-supply, regulator, PWM, PTP, PPS, PowerPC platform, clocks, resets, interrupts, GPIOs, pinctrl, power domains, and MFD parents consume the validated resources.

## State and Persistence Behavior
The file stores no mutable runtime state and writes no persistent data. Its persistence is ABI stability: compatible strings, required resource names, phandle/cell counts, child-node names, electrical limit properties, fallback ordering, and example layouts become long-lived contracts in DTS and DTB artifacts. Runtime state belongs to drivers and hardware after probe, including PWM duty cycle, fan enable/cooling state, thermal governor requests, and any tachometer/hwmon sampled state.

## Dependencies and Integration Points
Maintainers listed: Rahul Tanwar <rtanwar@maxlinear.com>. Direct schema dependencies are `pwm.yaml#`. Integration is through PWM providers, fan/hwmon/thermal drivers, GPIOs, clocks, and DTS thermal cooling maps. Broader integration points include the Linux PWM framework, hwmon/thermal fan consumers, GPIO/clock/regulator resources, and board fan-control nodes. Driver integration is through OF `compatible` matching and subsystem helpers; board integration is through DTS users that instantiate the compatible strings, child rails/channels, and phandle topology. Compatible scan signals found driver-side files: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/pwm/pwm-intel-lgm.c`. In-tree DTS scan signals found board users: no in-tree DTS users found by compatible scan. The file includes 1 embedded example for dt-schema validation.

## Risks
Primary risks are Devicetree ABI drift, incomplete compatible coverage, wrong fallback ordering, and resource-shape changes that still compile but break driver probe, power sequencing, clock setup, IRQ delivery, or consumer phandle resolution. Tightening required properties can reject existing board files; loosening them can hide missing supplies, clocks, resets, interrupts, current/voltage limits, PWM cell counts, PTP/PPS GPIOs, or regulator child constraints. This schema rejects unknown top-level properties with `additionalProperties: false`. It has 5 top-level properties and 5 distinct nested property names, so broad edits should be checked against all compatibles, examples, and in-tree DTS users rather than only the filename's nominal device.

## Test Signals
Run `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/pwm/intel,lgm-pwm.yaml` for targeted schema validation and `make dtbs_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/pwm/intel,lgm-pwm.yaml` against boards using these nodes. High-value negative checks remove required `compatible`, `reg`, interrupts, clocks, resets, supplies, GPIOs, child regulator/PWM/battery nodes, or phandles; use invalid cell counts or child `reg` values; misspell vendor properties; and exercise every compatible-specific branch. Runtime integration signals are successful driver probe, correct power_supply/regulator/PWM/PTP/PPS/platform registration, clean suspend/resume or runtime-PM behavior where applicable, valid interrupt/reset/power sequencing, and no dtbs_check regressions in embedded examples or in-tree DTS users.
<!-- END_FILE_RESEARCH: sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/pwm/intel,lgm-pwm.yaml -->
