Top-level required properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `clocks`; required properties collected from nested branches include `clocks`, `compatible`, `reg`. Patterned child schemas are none. Numeric and collection constraints include `maxItems=1`. Important resource roles: `compatible` is the stable hardware ABI and OF match key; `reg` gives MMIO, I2C, SPI, child address, or selector placement; `#pwm-cells` fixes child-node or phandle specifier shape; `clocks` binds functional, bus, reference, timer, or output clocks; `resets` defines reset-controller or GPIO reset sequencing. The highest-risk ABI details are compatible fallback ordering, register and clock layout, `#pwm-cells`, channel counts, period counter ranges, reset/interrupt resources, and SoC-specific polarity or prescaler rules.

## Control Flow
Control flow is dt-schema evaluation rather than imperative execution. `dt_binding_check` loads the core meta-schema, resolves `$ref` entries, applies `properties` and `patternProperties`, evaluates `allOf`, checks required fields and min/max constraints, validates examples, and enforces the closure policy. At runtime this YAML does not execute: the compiled DTB node is matched through a kernel OF table or subsystem-specific child-node parser, then helpers for power-supply, regulator, PWM, PTP, PPS, PowerPC platform, clocks, resets, interrupts, GPIOs, pinctrl, power domains, and MFD parents consume the validated resources.

## State and Persistence Behavior
The file stores no mutable runtime state and writes no persistent data. Its persistence is ABI stability: compatible strings, required resource names, phandle/cell counts, child-node names, electrical limit properties, fallback ordering, and example layouts become long-lived contracts in DTS and DTB artifacts. Runtime state belongs to drivers and hardware after probe, including PWM chip registration, channel allocation, configured period/duty/polarity, enabled outputs, clock/runtime-PM state, and hardware counter register state.

## Dependencies and Integration Points
Maintainers listed: Duje Mihanović <duje.mihanovic@skole.hr>. Direct schema dependencies are `pwm.yaml#`. Integration is through `drivers/pwm`, clock/reset/pinctrl/regulator providers, LED/backlight/fan/regulator consumers, and board DTS PWM phandles. Broader integration points include the Linux PWM framework, platform/MMIO/I2C/SPI PWM providers, clocks, resets, interrupts, pinctrl, power domains, and PWM consumer phandles. Driver integration is through OF `compatible` matching and subsystem helpers; board integration is through DTS users that instantiate the compatible strings, child rails/channels, and phandle topology. Compatible scan signals found driver-side files: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/pwm/pwm-pxa.c`. In-tree DTS scan signals found board users: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/intel/pxa/pxa25x.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/intel/pxa/pxa27x.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/intel/pxa/pxa3xx.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm64/boot/dts/marvell/mmp/pxa1908.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/riscv/boot/dts/spacemit/k1.dtsi`. The file includes 1 embedded example for dt-schema validation.

## Risks
Primary risks are Devicetree ABI drift, incomplete compatible coverage, wrong fallback ordering, and resource-shape changes that still compile but break driver probe, power sequencing, clock setup, IRQ delivery, or consumer phandle resolution. Tightening required properties can reject existing board files; loosening them can hide missing supplies, clocks, resets, interrupts, current/voltage limits, PWM cell counts, PTP/PPS GPIOs, or regulator child constraints. This schema rejects unknown top-level properties with `additionalProperties: false`. It has 5 top-level properties and 5 distinct nested property names, so broad edits should be checked against all compatibles, examples, and in-tree DTS users rather than only the filename's nominal device.

## Test Signals
Run `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/pwm/marvell,pxa-pwm.yaml` for targeted schema validation and `make dtbs_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/pwm/marvell,pxa-pwm.yaml` against boards using these nodes. High-value negative checks remove required `compatible`, `reg`, interrupts, clocks, resets, supplies, GPIOs, child regulator/PWM/battery nodes, or phandles; use invalid cell counts or child `reg` values; misspell vendor properties; and exercise every compatible-specific branch. Runtime integration signals are successful driver probe, correct power_supply/regulator/PWM/PTP/PPS/platform registration, clean suspend/resume or runtime-PM behavior where applicable, valid interrupt/reset/power sequencing, and no dtbs_check regressions in embedded examples or in-tree DTS users.

## Purpose
`sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/pwm/pwm-nexus-node.yaml` defines the Linux Devicetree YAML schema for `PWM Nexus node properties`, a PWM common-schema or nexus binding. The schema description says: Platforms can have a standardized connector/expansion slot that exposes PWMs signals to expansion boards. A nexus node allows to remap a phandle list in a consumer node through a connector node in a generic way. With this remapping, the consumer node needs to know only about the nexus node. Resources behind the nexus node are decoupled by the nexus node itself. It is declarative ABI documentation and validation input, not executable driver code. Top-level required properties are none declared at the top level; required properties collected from nested branches include none found. Patterned child schemas are none. Numeric and collection constraints include no explicit numeric or item limits collected. Important resource roles: `#pwm-cells` fixes child-node or phandle specifier shape; `pwm-map` describes PWM provider or consumer channel wiring; `pwm-map-mask` describes PWM provider or consumer channel wiring; `pwm-map-pass-thru` describes PWM provider or consumer channel wiring. The highest-risk ABI details are common cell counts, consumer specifier shape, channel numbering, period/polarity ABI, and child translation rules.

## Control Flow
Control flow is dt-schema evaluation rather than imperative execution. `dt_binding_check` loads the core meta-schema, resolves `$ref` entries, applies `properties` and `patternProperties`, evaluates `dependentRequired`, checks required fields and min/max constraints, validates examples, and enforces the closure policy. ## State and Persistence Behavior
The file stores no mutable runtime state and writes no persistent data. Its persistence is ABI stability: compatible strings, required resource names, phandle/cell counts, child-node names, electrical limit properties, fallback ordering, and example layouts become long-lived contracts in DTS and DTB artifacts. Runtime state belongs to drivers and hardware after probe, including PWM chip registration, channel request state, duty-cycle/period/polarity settings, enable state, and consumer-owned runtime policy.

## Purpose
`sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/pwm/pwm-rockchip.yaml` defines the Linux Devicetree YAML schema for `Rockchip PWM controller`, a PWM controller binding. It is declarative ABI documentation and validation input, not executable driver code. Its job is to keep DTS/DTB nodes aligned with the resources, child nodes, and electrical or timing constraints expected by the matching kernel subsystem before a driver probes hardware.

## Important APIs, Types, and Functions
The API surface is the schema contract: `$id`, `$schema`, `title`, `maintainers`, `properties`, `patternProperties`, `required`, composition keys, examples, and referenced common schemas. `compatible` uses `oneOf` with 7 branchs with 16 tokens: `rockchip,rk2928-pwm`, `rockchip,rk3288-pwm`, `rockchip,rk3328-pwm`, `rockchip,vop-pwm`, `rockchip,rk3036-pwm`, `rockchip,rk3128-pwm`, `rockchip,rk3368-pwm`, `rockchip,rk3399-pwm`, `rockchip,rv1108-pwm`, `rockchip,px30-pwm`, `rockchip,rk3308-pwm`, `rockchip,rk3528-pwm`, `rockchip,rk3562-pwm`, `rockchip,rk3568-pwm`, `rockchip,rk3588-pwm`, `rockchip,rv1126-pwm`. Top-level properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `clocks`, `clock-names`, `#pwm-cells`. Top-level required properties are `compatible`, `reg`; required properties collected from nested branches include `clock-names`, `clocks`, `compatible`, `reg`. Patterned child schemas are none. Numeric and collection constraints include `maxItems=1`, `minItems=1`, `maxItems=2`. Important resource roles: `compatible` is the stable hardware ABI and OF match key; `reg` gives MMIO, I2C, SPI, child address, or selector placement; `clocks` binds functional, bus, reference, timer, or output clocks; `clock-names` binds functional, bus, reference, timer, or output clocks; `#pwm-cells` fixes child-node or phandle specifier shape. The highest-risk ABI details are compatible fallback ordering, register and clock layout, `#pwm-cells`, channel counts, period counter ranges, reset/interrupt resources, and SoC-specific polarity or prescaler rules.

## Control Flow
Control flow is dt-schema evaluation rather than imperative execution. `dt_binding_check` loads the core meta-schema, resolves `$ref` entries, applies `properties` and `patternProperties`, evaluates `allOf`, checks required fields and min/max constraints, validates examples, and enforces the closure policy. At runtime this YAML does not execute: the compiled DTB node is matched through a kernel OF table or subsystem-specific child-node parser, then helpers for power-supply, regulator, PWM, PTP, PPS, PowerPC platform, clocks, resets, interrupts, GPIOs, pinctrl, power domains, and MFD parents consume the validated resources.

## State and Persistence Behavior
The file stores no mutable runtime state and writes no persistent data. Its persistence is ABI stability: compatible strings, required resource names, phandle/cell counts, child-node names, electrical limit properties, fallback ordering, and example layouts become long-lived contracts in DTS and DTB artifacts. Runtime state belongs to drivers and hardware after probe, including PWM chip registration, channel allocation, configured period/duty/polarity, enabled outputs, clock/runtime-PM state, and hardware counter register state.

## Dependencies and Integration Points
Maintainers listed: Heiko Stuebner <heiko@sntech.de>. Direct schema dependencies are `pwm.yaml#`. Integration is through `drivers/pwm`, clock/reset/pinctrl/regulator providers, LED/backlight/fan/regulator consumers, and board DTS PWM phandles. Broader integration points include the Linux PWM framework, platform/MMIO/I2C/SPI PWM providers, clocks, resets, interrupts, pinctrl, power domains, and PWM consumer phandles. Driver integration is through OF `compatible` matching and subsystem helpers; board integration is through DTS users that instantiate the compatible strings, child rails/channels, and phandle topology. Compatible scan signals found driver-side files: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/pwm/pwm-rockchip.c`. In-tree DTS scan signals found board users: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/rockchip/rk3036.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/rockchip/rk3128.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/rockchip/rk322x.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/rockchip/rk3288.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/rockchip/rk3xxx.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/rockchip/rv1108.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/rockchip/rv1126.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm64/boot/dts/rockchip/px30.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm64/boot/dts/rockchip/rk3308.dtsi`, and 1 more. The file includes 1 embedded example for dt-schema validation.

## Risks
Primary risks are Devicetree ABI drift, incomplete compatible coverage, wrong fallback ordering, and resource-shape changes that still compile but break driver probe, power sequencing, clock setup, IRQ delivery, or consumer phandle resolution. Tightening required properties can reject existing board files; loosening them can hide missing supplies, clocks, resets, interrupts, current/voltage limits, PWM cell counts, PTP/PPS GPIOs, or regulator child constraints. This schema rejects unknown top-level properties with `additionalProperties: false`. It has 5 top-level properties and 5 distinct nested property names, so broad edits should be checked against all compatibles, examples, and in-tree DTS users rather than only the filename's nominal device.

## Test Signals
Run `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/pwm/pwm-rockchip.yaml` for targeted schema validation and `make dtbs_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/pwm/pwm-rockchip.yaml` against boards using these nodes. High-value negative checks remove required `compatible`, `reg`, interrupts, clocks, resets, supplies, GPIOs, child regulator/PWM/battery nodes, or phandles; use invalid cell counts or child `reg` values; misspell vendor properties; and exercise every compatible-specific branch. Runtime integration signals are successful driver probe, correct power_supply/regulator/PWM/PTP/PPS/platform registration, clean suspend/resume or runtime-PM behavior where applicable, valid interrupt/reset/power sequencing, and no dtbs_check regressions in embedded examples or in-tree DTS users.

# sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/pwm/pwm-samsung.yaml

## Purpose
`sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/pwm/pwm-samsung.yaml` defines the Linux Devicetree YAML schema for `Samsung SoC PWM timers`, a PWM controller binding. The schema description says: Samsung SoCs contain PWM timer blocks which can be used for system clock source and clock event timers, as well as to drive SoC outputs with PWM signal. Each PWM timer block provides 5 PWM channels (not all of them can drive physical outputs - see SoC and board manual). Be aware that the clocksource driver supports only uniprocessor systems. It is declarative ABI documentation and validation input, not executable driver code. Its job is to keep DTS/DTB nodes aligned with the resources, child nodes, and electrical or timing constraints expected by the matching kernel subsystem before a driver probes hardware.

# sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/pwm/pwm-sifive.yaml

## Purpose
`sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/pwm/pwm-sifive.yaml` defines the Linux Devicetree YAML schema for `SiFive PWM controller`, a PWM controller binding. The schema description says: Unlike most other PWM controllers, the SiFive PWM controller currently only supports one period for all channels in the PWM. All PWMs need to run at the same period. The period also has significant restrictions on the values it can achieve, which the driver rounds to the nearest achievable period. PWM RTL that corresponds to the IP block version numbers can be found here - https://github.com/sifive/sifive-blocks/tree/master/src/main/scala/devices/pwm It is declarative ABI documentation and validation input, not executable driver code. # sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/pwm/renesas,rzg2l-gpt.yaml

## Purpose
`sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/pwm/renesas,rzg2l-gpt.yaml` defines the Linux Devicetree YAML schema for `Renesas RZ/G2L General PWM Timer (GPT)`, a PWM controller binding. The schema description says: RZ/G2L General PWM Timer (GPT) composed of 8 channels with 32-bit timer (GPT32E). It supports the following functions * 32 bits x 8 channels. * Up-counting or down-counting (saw waves) or up/down-counting (triangle waves) for each counter. * Clock sources independently selectable for each channel. * Two I/O pins per channel. * Two output compare/input capture registers per channel. * For the two output compare/input capture registers of each channel, four registers are provided as buffer registers and are capable of operating as comparison registers when buffering is not in use. * In output compare operation,... The schema description says: This describes the DesignWare APB timers module when used in the PWM mode. The IP core can be generated with various options which can control the functionality, the number of PWMs available and other internal controls the designer requires. The IP block has a version register so this can be used for detection instead of having to encode the IP version number in the device tree compatible. Patterned child schemas are none. Numeric and collection constraints include `maxItems=1`.
